## [2026-08-04] — via Kaito (asleep, v41 council §4A) — SECURITY STRIP + GATE FIX: Team Room DELETED from the app @ `1dc90a4`
- Asked: execute the v41 root-migration council's §4 ruling — the in-app Team Room is a LIVE hole in the deployed
  customer build and outranks the landing-page question. (A) delete the viewer IIFE + `#teamRoomCard` markup entirely
  (re-pointing the URL is NOT enough), keep `team-chat.html` in source but never published; (E) fix the gate's coverage
  in the SAME commit — add a `raw.githubusercontent` assertion to `green.js` §13 and drop the dead `team-chat.html`
  entry from `PUBLISHED_TEXT`. My standing security exception: I code this one directly. Baseline tip `7f4a875`.
- **THE HOLE, PLAINLY — AND IT WAS MINE.** The card was documented (by me, repeatedly) as "owner-gated". The gate was
  `ownerFile || localStorage.mrln_team`, and the flag was set by *anything* matching `/team/i` in `location.hash`:
  `try{ if(/team/i.test(location.hash)) localStorage.setItem('mrln_team','1'); }catch(e){}`. Typing `mrln.online/#team`
  was the entire attack. The `#__ownerKeySrc` arm really is byte-empty in every published file (preflight enforces it),
  so the hash flag was the ONLY live path — and it **persists in localStorage**, leaving the internal chat card
  permanently in that customer's app, polling `raw.githubusercontent.com` every 5s. Consequences: (1) the customer build
  phoned home, undercutting FREE · OFFLINE; (2) my own recorded audit line — "Outbound vectors enumerated: ONLY fetch =
  Team Room, OWNER-GATED → customer files never fetch" (2026-07-02 full-surface audit, repeated in several later
  sign-offs) — was **FALSE**. I wrote it, I repeated it, and I did not re-derive the gate when I re-asserted it. On a
  paid privacy product a false published privacy claim is refund/consumer-protection exposure, not embarrassment.
  LESSON, concrete: "owner-gated" is a claim about a BOOLEAN EXPRESSION, and I must re-read the expression every time I
  restate it — I had been carrying the 2026-06-28 reading forward for five weeks. I had even logged the `#team` flag as
  a "Low, owner-only impact" backlog nit on 2026-06-28; I under-rated it because I scored it as *information exposure*
  (only the public chat) and never scored it as *the app phoning home*, which is the actual product claim.
- DID (index.html, -56 lines, ZERO additions — verified `git diff --numstat` = `0 56`):
  - deleted the whole `<script>` block "Owner-only Team Room viewer" (was 9742-9791): RAW const, `#team` hash sniff,
    `mrln_team` flag, `tick()` 5s `fetch` poll, feed renderer, open/close button wiring.
  - deleted `#teamRoomCard` markup (was 2438-2443) inside `<section class="panel" id="connect">`.
  - RESIDUE GREPS ALL **0**: `teamRoomCard` · `teamRoomOpen` · `teamRoomFeed` · `mrln_team` · `raw.githubusercontent` ·
    `Team Room`. i18n: the card carried `data-i18n-skip` and the dictionary has NO "Team Room" key (verified, did not
    assume) → no orphan keys, MISSING unchanged. No CSS rule referenced any of the ids (0 hits) → no orphaned rule.
    `exportHTML`/`exportBlank` never referenced it → export paths untouched. `team-chat.html` left in source, untouched,
    and it is NOT in `tools/publish/deploy_map.json` → never enters the deploy set.
- DID (tools/release/green.js):
  - NEW section **13b phone-home scan**: no published file may contain `raw.githubusercontent`. Deliberate design call —
    the file list is **DERIVED from `tools/publish/deploy_map.json`** (the single source of truth for the deploy set,
    Kaito's phase-0 artifact) rather than hand-listed, and it **FAILS CLOSED** if the map is missing/unparseable. That
    fixes the CLASS (a gate list drifting from the deploy set), not just the instance. Binary extensions skipped.
  - DROPPED `team-chat.html` from `PUBLISHED_TEXT`. **Correction to Kaito's brief:** it does NOT print
    "(not present — skipped)" — the file EXISTS in source, so the scan ran on it and printed "✓ team-chat.html clean".
    The coverage was fake in a different way than described: a reassuring green tick for a file that does not publish.
- VERIFIED, NOT ASSUMED:
  - `node tools/release/green.js` → **GREEN exit 0** (all suites; html-parse now **3/3** blocks — one fewer because I
    deleted a `<script>`; parser 21/21; preflight CLEAR ×3; PII guard 17/17; leak scan 3 files clean; phone-home scan
    "11 published text files — no raw.githubusercontent reference"; APP_VER v40 === sw v40). Re-ran AFTER rebasing onto
    Kaito's `3561e09` → still exit 0.

## [2026-08-04] — via Kaito (asleep, step 10, RELEASE A gate, 4th re-sign) — SAFE @ `58127f4` (chat tip `6da449f`)

Nothing else in the whole 61-file set (`sw.js`/`manifest`/`landing`/`legal`/`GUIDE`/`README`/`assets` diff **EMPTY**). Per-commit, linear: `6225b5c..8f6c65f` 7/2 · `8f6c65f..6f2ee1d` 2/2 · `6f2ee1d..58127f4` 1/1 → **10/5 since my last SAFE**.

  - `.nojekyll`: `git rev-parse origin/gh-pages:.nojekyll` = `e69de29` (**the canonical empty blob**), `cat-file -s` = **0**, and `git log origin/gh-pages -- .nojekyll` shows it has been live since **`671698b` (2026-07-29)**. So `3561e09` added it to **SOURCE**, not to the published site. **My "NEW published file" claim was wrong** — it was new in the source tree only, which is precisely what lets `deploy.js --check` reproduce the live tree (path set identical, 61 files). Kaito's "zero published bytes" stands. Correcting my own record.
  - index.html 69/63 vs his 70/64: confirmed **69/63** at `6225b5c` — he was quoting index+CNAME combined. My correction stands, as he says.
  - LESSON FOR ME: "new file in the diff" ≠ "new published file". The authority for *published* is `origin/gh-pages`, not the source branch. I asserted from the source diff alone.
- **(1) THE LOOKAHEADS — I FALSIFIED KAITO'S CLAIM. Two real regressions, both low severity, NOT gating.** He claimed `fizetés(?!i)`/`bér(?!l)` "cannot make any currently-working phrase stop working." Ran the SHIPPED `parseClause` extracted from both `6f2ee1d` and the tip over a 48-case hu corpus:
  - **`a fizetési szintem 25000`** (my pay LEVEL) and **`fizetési sávom 25000`** (my pay BAND) were `setIncome` at `6f2ee1d` and are `addItem` at the tip. So yes — Hungarian income phrasings whose very next letter after `fizetés` is `i` **do exist**; `fizetési` is the productive adjectival suffix. His absolute is false.
  - WHY IT DOESN'T GATE: (a) both forms are stilted for a quick-update box — the four natural forms (`fizetésem`, `bérem`, `jövedelmem`, `bevételem`) all work, and **two of them only work because of this commit**; (b) the failure lands in `addItem` behind the **explicit Apply preview** (`sendBtn`→`showProposal`, `aiApply` click required, @6109-6134 — verified by reading the wiring), so it is a visible wrong suggestion, not a silent write; (c) the counterfactual: what is LIVE today turns **`bérleti díj 6000` (RENT) into `setIncome 6000`**, which corrupts leftover/tier/affordability/every projection. Holding the release keeps the worse behaviour in customers' hands. Same reasoning I recorded for `_priceFrom` at `6225b5c`.
  - HIS REASONING, CORRECTED PRECISELY: an allowlist fails toward `addItem` for *every* suffix you forget; a lookahead fails toward `addItem` for *every* phrase whose next letter is `i`/`l`, known or not. The lookahead is **narrower**, which is the right call — but it is **not** "can only remove known collisions." Direction of failure is identical; only the blast radius differs.
  - VERIFIED HIS SIDE HOLDS TOO: the app's own hu UI words `Előfizetés`/`Előfizetések` (SUBSCRIPTIONS) and `Lakbér` (RENT) were never at risk — the LEADING boundary `(?<![\p{L}\p{N}])` already blocks them (`ő`/`k` precede the stem). `bérplafon` (wage ceiling) still matches, correctly. No hu income word begins `bérl`. `bevétel`/`jövedelm` introduce no new collision (`bevásárlás` diverges at char 3; all `bevétel*`/`jövedelm*` forms are revenue-related).
  - `LEX.income` is interpolated in **exactly one** place (`_has(LEX.income, c)` @5829) — no composition with `_w()`, so the inline lookaheads cannot leak into a differently-anchored regex. Checked, not assumed.
- **(2) ===== THE COLLISION CLASS IS SYSTEMIC AND LIVE IN EVERY LANGUAGE — my biggest find this run. ALL PRE-EXISTING (identical at `46ca898`), so it does NOT gate — but Kaito asked, and the answer is "far more than the two Mikoto surfaced." =====** Differential `46ca898` vs tip over a hand-built cross-language corpus; every one of these is what the SHIPPED parser does **today**:
  - **hu, `bér` collisions `(?!l)` does NOT close:** `bérautó 900` (rental CAR) · `bérmunka` · `bérgarázs` · `bérkocsi` → all **`setIncome`**. `jövedelemadó 4000` (income **TAX**) → `setIncome` (was already so at base; the new `jövedelm` stem adds the misspelling `jövedelmadó` too).
  - **de:** **`Lohnsteuer 4000`** (income TAX, an expense) → `setIncome`. `Gehaltskonto 30` (salary-account fee) → `setIncome`. `Kreditkarte 79` → **`setLoanPayment`**.
  - **sv:** **`viktigt möte 200`** / `ett viktigt köp 150` / `viktig faktura 300` → **`setBody.weight`** (`vikt` prefixes `viktig`, the most common adjective in the language). `Viktoria 75` → weight. `lönsamt köp 400` (profitable purchase) and `lönnsirap 60` (maple syrup) → `setIncome`.
  - **da/nb:** `Lønstrup ferie 2000` (a town) · `lønkonto gebyr 25` · `lønnstilskudd 500` → `setIncome`. `tallerken 120` / `tallerkener 120` (plates) and bare `tall 180` (nb for *number*) → **`setBody.height`**. `lånebevis 100` → `setLoanPayment`.
  - **es: a DIFFERENT and worse bug — the AGE rule has NO leading boundary at all.** @5812 composes `'(?:'+LEX.age+')'+_WA` — trailing boundary only, so `edad` matches at the END of any word: **`propiedad 41`** / `sociedad 45` / `la propiedad es 41` → **`setProfile.age`**. Every other `_has()` group is leading-anchored; this one is trailing-anchored, which is strictly worse (suffix match). Also `ahorrar 500 en la mesa` → `setSavingsMatch` (`mes` prefixes `mesa`), `peso 80` → weight.
  - SEVERITY, honestly: all land behind the Apply preview with a human-readable description, so none is a silent write. But `setIncome`/`setBody`/`setLoanPayment` are one careless tap from corrupting the finance and health models, and **the words involved are ordinary vocabulary, not edge cases**. Routed to Kaito as a **structural** item, not a patch item: `_has()`'s leading-boundary-only contract is wrong for agglutinative (hu) and compounding (de/sv/da/nb) morphology, and the age rule's missing leading boundary is a separate one-token bug. Kaito's own note (RELEASE A, "(b) is a patch, not the structural answer") is correct and I am confirming it with measurements.
- **(3) SECURITY DIMENSION OF A WRONG `setIncome` — ruled: NONE. The tamper story is untouched.** Traced the whole path rather than reasoning: `parseClause`→`showProposal`→ user clicks `#aiApply` @6123 →`applyChange` @5650 →`setIncome` @4226 → `MODEL.incomeGuess` → `recomputeIncome()` → `MODEL.income`. Every downstream read is display/compute and is token-gated at the point of computation, not at the point of storage: `leftOver(income) = __sys.token()*income − GRAND − loanAmt()` @3101, `comfort()` @3102 (a second `token()`), `itemMonthly`/`groupTotal`/`recomputeGrand` unchanged. `__sys.token()` returns **1 or NaN** @3051 — a **multiplier**, so a wrong-but-finite income yields a wrong-but-finite figure on a legitimate copy and **NaN on a tampered one, exactly as before**. Correctness of the operand and integrity of the multiplier are orthogonal. Grepped every `MODEL.income`/`incomeGuess` read site (24 of them): **not one is inside `__sys`, `arm`, `trip`, `isArmed`, `isTripped`, `_ecVerify`, PUBCHK or the PUB_B64 watchdog** — no watchdog consumes income, so no income value can suppress a trip or un-poison a figure. `morningBriefing` @2918 is the only new-ish consumer and it is explicitly `isFinite(surplus)`-gated on the poisoned `leftOver`. **A corrupted income is a user-data integrity defect, not a licensing/anti-tamper weakening.** I will not inflate it into a security finding.

Comment text contains **no** `"`, `'`, `<`, `>`, `&`, `*/` or `</` ⇒ cannot break the single-quoted JS string literals, the `style="…"` attributes, or the `<script>` block.

It is **deletion-only** (`replace(…,'')`/`(…,' ')`) so **it cannot introduce a character that was not in the input**, and the three empty-string strips are anchored (`^`/`$`) so they remove a prefix/suffix and **cannot fuse two interior fragments** into a new token; every interior strip substitutes a space. ## [2026-08-06] — via Kaito (asleep, DESIGN) — TASK #22 SPLIT-REPO DESIGN — ruling: INVERT the split (site does NOT move)

Cover target shape, CNAME/Pages/DNS cutover with zero downtime (SW scope/cache across the cutover), the history question (Osefe's prior: rewriting is worthless post-exposure), `deploy.js`/`deploy_map` retarget without paid CI, sequencing vs task #21 (root migration), and an honest risk ranking. **DESIGN ONLY — no migration executed.** Deliver spec + plain-language summary.
- **DELIVERABLE: `team/security/split-repo-design.md` (361 lines, 11 sections) + a TEAM-CHAT MESSAGES post.** Read-only throughout: **no lock, no app-code edit, no repo/DNS/Pages change.**
- ===== **I MEASURED THE EXPOSURE INSTEAD OF INHERITING THE SUMMARY, AND BOTH MY INVARIANTS HELD FOR THE ENTIRE PUBLIC LIFE OF THE REPO.** =====
  - **Full-history key scan over ALL 1,128 commits on ALL refs** (`MII[A-Za-z0-9+/]{40,}`, `sk_live_…`, `whsec_…`, PEM bodies with a real base64 tail) → **ZERO hits.** The 8-pattern pickaxe found `BEGIN PRIVATE KEY` in exactly 2 commits (`7d6ef4a`, `2fc919b`) — **both are my own log/chat entries quoting the marker string in a "0 private-key markers" line**, not key material; I opened both rather than report a scary number.

  - **`gh-pages` (143 commits) has NEVER contained `team/`, `TEAM-CHAT.md`, `tools/` or `CLAUDE.md`** (`git log origin/gh-pages -- <those paths>` = empty), and its tree today is **exactly the 74 `deploy_map.json` entries**. The published branch was already deploy-only; there is nothing to extract.
- ===== **I CORRECTED THE BRIEF'S LOAD-BEARING CLAIM — "verified no keys/PII" IS NOT ACCURATE.** ===== `Miradiosefe@gmail.com` / `osefemiradi@gmail.com` occur **42 times** in world-readable files (`TEAM-CHAT.md`, 4 team logs, `tools/i18n/landing_keys.json:186` — a stale i18n key from before the contact swap at `488edf3` — and `tools/test/preflight_pii_test.js` where it is a necessary fixture). **Published set = 0 hits, verified programmatically over all 74 entries** (published contact is `Kontaktmrln@gmail.com` ×12; `Osefe Miradi` appears in `legal.html` ×5 / `landing.html` ×1 / `index.html` ×2 **by design** — provider identity is legally required). **This is the highest-severity item and the split does NOT fix it:** it is the address on the account that owns the domain + repo + Stripe ⇒ a successful phish costs all three. Routed to @Osefe as **2FA/passkeys, 15 minutes, ahead of the split.**
- ===== **THE RULING: INVERT THE SPLIT. THE SITE DOES NOT MOVE.** ===== Kaito's sketch (this repo → private, new public repo serves) is the expensive half of a trade with a free half available. **Keep `sageMrln/my-first-repo` public and deploy-only; move the workshop to a new private `mrln-dev`.** Then the cutover risk is **deleted, not mitigated** — no Pages setting, no DNS, no CNAME, no cert re-issue, no SW/cache/manifest/scope change, because **the origin never changes**. Four measured reasons: (1) `gh-pages` is already clean (above); (2) **`GUIDE.md:4` publishes `https://sagemrln.github.io/my-first-repo/` to customers — a SECOND ORIGIN**, so moving or renaming the repo silently strands any data that origin's `localStorage` holds, with no error and no recovery from the new URL — real customer harm for zero security gain; (3) **DNS needs no change either way** (measured: apex A → `185.199.108–111.153`, GitHub anycast; no `www` CNAME; TXT is SPF only) — the exclusive binding is GitHub-side, and re-attaching a custom domain triggers a fresh Let's Encrypt issuance ⇒ a minutes-to-an-hour TLS window; (4) deleting a branch cannot break a running site. **Cost of inverting: the public repo keeps its already-crawled history and its ugly name — neither is worth a cert window. Explicit NO on renaming** (breaks `GUIDE.md:4` + forces a Pages rebuild).
- **HISTORY — I AGREE WITH @Osefe AND SAID WHY, RATHER THAN JUST AGREEING.** Do not rewrite. It cannot un-publish what forks/GH Archive/Software Heritage/code-search already hold, GitHub keeps unreferenced objects reachable by SHA on its own schedule — **and a rewrite would dangle every SHA our sign-off discipline depends on** (hundreds, hand-written across chat, 5 logs, 6 council rulings), i.e. paying with the integrity of our audit trail for a cosmetic result. **And there is nothing to redact** — the three scans above prove it. The only real forward win is deleting the dev branches so nothing NEW publishes; that is not retroactive and I said so.
- ===== **PREREQUISITE FINDING I FOUND WHILE MODELLING THE CUTOVER → @Kaito (matters to #21 as much as #22).** ===== **`sw.js:26-29` (document) and `:35-38` (assets) cache whatever the fetch resolves to, with no status check.** A dead network rejects → `.catch` handles it, fine. **But a 404/503 does NOT reject, and `Cache.put()` only refuses 206 / error / opaqueredirect — a 404 body is perfectly cacheable.** So in any window where the origin answers with the wrong thing (Pages cutover, unattached domain, GitHub incident, mid-deploy tree) the user's cache entry for `/` is overwritten with GitHub's 404 page and **their OFFLINE launch serves that 404 from then on** — `localStorage` intact, app apparently bricked, no user-side recovery. Fix is 2 lines, both branches: `if (resp && resp.ok && resp.type === 'basic')` before the `c.put`. **I did NOT claim my security exception** — it is `sw.js` robustness, so it is Kaito's; pairs with `routing_test`'s phase-2 `pathname` guard on the same branch. Hard prerequisite if the naive Pages-move is ever chosen.
- **DEPLOY RETARGET — exactly ONE code change.** `tools/publish/deploy.js:29` hardcodes `const BRANCH = 'origin/gh-pages'`; after the split `origin` is the private repo and `--check` dies at `:73`. → `process.env.MRLN_LIVE_REF || 'origin/gh-pages'` (default-preserving, safe to land today). Release step stays free + manual: `git remote add live …` → `git fetch live gh-pages` → `MRLN_LIVE_REF=live/gh-pages deploy.js --check` → `deploy.js --out /tmp/mrln-live` → copy into a scratch `gh-pages` clone → push. **Checked rather than assumed: `green.js` does NOT invoke `deploy.js --check`** (it runs `routing_test.js` at `green.js:137-138`, which reads the map + files, never a git ref) ⇒ the split cannot turn the gate RED — **which also means nothing would tell us if that manual step silently stopped running.** Flagged as a loud runbook item. `deploy_map.json` itself needs no change (repo-agnostic; `routing_test.js:58`'s 74-pin still holds). **And I stated which control actually does the work: the split is NOT what keeps internal files off the live site — `deploy_map` + `--out` + `preflight` is** (an explicit allowlist that cannot copy an undeclared file; it is what closed `2964d70`'s `team-chat.html`). Two hard rules for the push script: materialise from the map, and **never `git push live HEAD:gh-pages`** — that one command republishes the whole workshop and this design is the first time it becomes typo-able.
- **SEQUENCING vs #21:** they share the publish path so never concurrent, but the inversion moves **no published byte and no Pages config** ⇒ no conflict. **#22 first, in a gap or before phase 1 opens** (short, reversible; the `BRANCH` change is far safer landed at phase 0 than mid-migration). If the naive split is ever chosen instead, it must come **after** #21 — never two Pages-level risks stacked.
- **RISK RANKING, HONEST:** phishing on the owner account (**HIGH — and not fixed by the split**) > licence-cracking convenience (MEDIUM-business; 3 in-repo `arm()` bypass sites — `tools/shots/generate.js:153-154`, `tools/test/parity_harness.js:65-68` and `:151` — plus my logs' written inventory of live unfixed weaknesses; **bounded, because the whole anti-tamper is client-side in a file every customer downloads — our notes save an attacker an evening, they grant no capability**) > competitive/strategy leak (MEDIUM) > owner name/city (LOW; on the legal page by design) > **customer data: NONE, ever** (app is local-only; nothing customer-side has ever been in this repo). **Verdict: hygiene with a business tail, NOT urgent, should not jump the reskin queue.** Effort ~1.5 h, no downtime, every step revertible.
- **VERIFICATION LIMIT I RECORDED RATHER THAN PAPERED OVER:** I could not independently re-confirm visibility from this box — `api.github.com` → **403 at the agent proxy**, and `GH_TOKEN`/`GITHUB_TOKEN` are in the env with git config injection, so my `raw.githubusercontent` **200 proves nothing** (same inconclusive result I logged for six weeks; `github.com/<nonexistent-repo>` also returned 403, so that control is worthless too). I take @Kaito's API metadata read as the working fact and named it as his measurement, not mine. **@Osefe's logged-out browser check outranks both of us.**

- 🟡 **NEW FINDING (mine, non-security, routed not blocked): the arithmetic UNDERFLOWS for an off-viewport target.** `if(L<4){W-=(4-L);L=4;}` gives `W = r.left + r.width + 4` ⇒ **negative when the element is entirely left of the viewport**; symmetrically `H<0` when it is entirely above. Simulated: off-screen-left → **W −346**; off-screen-top → **H −256**. CSSOM drops the invalid value, so the ring silently retains the PREVIOUS step's size at `left:4`. **Reachability is real but benign:** `header.hud` is `position:relative` (scrolls) and the tour's `follow` re-runs `reposition()` on every scroll, so scrolling ≳48px past the top on the `#hdrGear` step reaches it; `place()` also `scrollIntoView`s the target, so it self-corrects on the next step. **Cosmetic only — no security axis. Fix (Stage-5 docket): `W=Math.max(40,W); H=Math.max(40,H);` after the two branches.** Recorded honestly: the previous translate-based clamp did NOT have this failure mode (it moved the ring into view instead) — but the translate behaviour is precisely what Arthur ruled out, so this is the right trade, not a regression to reverse.

- ===== 🔴 **(7) THE GUIDE — THE INCIDENT-#4 CORRECTION INTRODUCED SEVEN NEW FABRICATED CAPABILITY CLAIMS. I VERIFIED EACH AT THE CODE, AND THE PUBLISHED HERO SCREENSHOT REFUTES TWO OF THEM ON ITS FACE.** =====
  Kaito asked me for exactly this ("verify the correction didn't introduce new claims"). It did. All seven are NEW in `244d2a6`, in `GUIDE.md` §"📍 The Dashboard" and its rebuilt PDF:
  - **G1 — "Auto-log from CSV — import a CSV file (date · weight columns) to backfill history." FALSE.** `importWeightCSV()` iterates the rows and keeps **ONE** value (`best` = the latest dated row); the `#impWeight` handler then does `STATE.body.weight=w.weight; logWeight(w.weight, w.date)` — **a single upsert** — and toasts *"Set your weight to {w} kg"*. **It does not backfill anything.**
  - **G2 — "the Home card shows your delta since 30 days ago (or the last entry if fewer than 30 days exist)." FALSE.** The card computes `wl[last].kg − wl[last-1].kg` and labels it `since {previous entry's date}`. **There is no 30-day window anywhere in the file.**
  - **G3 — "Weight — current weight, your target, delta…" FALSE on "your target".** The card renders value + delta only, and the code comment is explicit: *"P9 (Arthur ruling): NO direction colouring — **the app has no weight goal**"*. The guide invents a goal the app deliberately does not have.
  - **G4 — "Workouts — workouts this week, your weekly plan target, upcoming sessions in the sub-line." FALSE on all three.** `kcWo = String((STATE.workouts||[]).length)` (the whole plan, not this week), the scope label is `In plan`, and the sub-line is the literal `t('plan · no dates yet')` — sitting directly under the comment *"P14: honest about V1 — plan entries carry no dates."* **The guide re-adds precisely the claim the code refused to make.**
  - **G5 — "a mini-chart (if enabled) showing your last 6 months." WRONG TWICE.** `incomeChartSVG` draws `incomeEntries().slice(-INCOME_WINDOW)` and **`INCOME_WINDOW = 12`**. The demo shot shows 6 bars because the seed has 6 months, not because the window is 6 — the guide generalised from a screenshot. And there is no "enable": `wrap.style.display = svg ? So the `persisted=true` branch was never reached by a genuine browser sequence in this environment. What I did establish:
  - **REAL, and it is the regression that mattered:** a normal load fires `pageshow{persisted:false}` and the `if(ev.persisted)` guard means **no second loop is armed** — post-back draw rate matched a single loop. The obvious way this refactor could have gone wrong is closed.
  - **SYNTHETIC** (dispatched `PageTransitionEvent`s): baseline **7 draws/s** → `pagehide{persisted:true}` → **0** (loop parked, GL **not** disposed) → `pageshow{persisted:true}` → **8** (re-armed). The handler does what Kaito says.
  - **ADVERSARIAL:** a **second** `pageshow{persisted:true}` with no intervening `pagehide` → **14 draws/s = two loops, double GPU**. **Per spec a persisted `pageshow` can only follow a persisted `pagehide`, so I could not produce this from any real browser sequence** — it is a missing idempotency guard, **not a live bug**. One-token fix if wanted: `cancelAnimationFrame(raf)` before re-arming. **NOT blocking.**
  - **Real-unload path:** `pagehide{persisted:false}` disposes GL with **0 page errors**; body text 8,256 chars, CTA present, hero opacity 1, cursor still `fixed` ⇒ **disposal never removes content.** `t0` is not reset on restore so the world resumes at a later rotation — a visual jump, correct-looking, not a defect.
